Approve it again in two minutes.` What `obid` described, and when, is kept with the job, across restarts. This is a narrowing, not a seal: signing the commit itself is #553. While a publish by hand runs, the job takes no words (`POST /v1/jobs/{id}/messages`, `422`) and can't be dismissed (`409`). The refusals are `403`: `That device isn't registered with this runner.`, `The signature doesn't match the device's key.`, `The device approved something else. Approve it again.`, `The approval is more than two minutes old. Approve it again.` and `That approval was already used. Approve it again.`. A publish that fails answers `409` with its line.
- **The journal.** `obid`'s log has one line per thing it sent (`job <id>: sent push <repo> <branch> <commit>`), ids only, and one line per publish that didn't go out, saying why (never a secret).

### obid only: failures

`obid` keeps [Failure records](#failure-record) and serves [`GET /v1/failures`](#get-v1failureslimit50), [`GET /v1/failures/{id}`](#get-v1failuresid) and [`POST /v1/failures/{id}/fix`](#post-v1failuresidfix) with every key above (#445). What is particular to `obid`:

- **What it records.** Each time a job changes, `obid` looks at it as the Swift runner does (Engine/Failures.swift, `found`): a `failed` job is `limit` when it stopped at a limit, `publishError` when its summary starts `Not published:`, and `failed` otherwise; a `done` job with `publishError` set is `publishError`. Only while it is news (it ended within a day), and once: the record's id is `<job>-<trigger>-<when it ended, in seconds>`, and a record is never written again. `obid` looks only as a job changes: it has no sweep, and doesn't look at ended jobs again when it starts, so a failure is missed if `obid` stops between keeping the job and writing its record (#571). A job the owner stopped isn't a failure, and `obid` leaves no job `interrupted` (a restart carries its jobs on), so `interrupted` is never recorded. The other triggers need what `obid` doesn't run (Oby's review, merging, the minute's sweep, the brain's fallback) and are never recorded: `reviewCap`, `noVerdict`, `noReviewer`, `checkError`, `unnamedFailure`, `mergeError`, `flakyTest`, `stuck`, `brainFallback`, and the interventions `handMerge` and `outsideCommit` (a person merging is how `obid` works, never an intervention). `intervention` is always false.
- **Where.** One file per record, `home/failures/<id>.json` (0600, in a 0700 folder), never pruned. `GET /v1/failures/{id}` takes only ids `obid` makes (letters, digits and dashes): anything else is `404`, like an id it doesn't have (`There's no failure <id>.`). `limit` is a whole number (`422` `limit is a number.` otherwise, as `GET /v1/jobs`), below 0 is none and above 1,000 is 1,000.
- **The evidence**, with every secret `obid` knows taken out (its API token, its Obi key, the Sphere's delegation and token, and the vault's once it is ported, #457: `crate::secrets`), and so are `title` and `what` (a limit names the check's command): `summary` is the end of the job's report (4,000 characters), `step` its stage, phase, step and round, `check` the end of what its last check printed (`home/logs/<job>/check.log`, 3,000 characters) and `Publish: <publishError>`, or null; a `check.log` written before a failure of the job `obid` recorded already is an earlier run's and isn't quoted. `reviews` and `log` are always empty (`obid` runs no review of its own, and keeps no log lines in memory), `worktree` and `source` always null (both are read at the post-mortem).
- **No post-mortem yet.** `obid` runs no post-mortem turn, so `postMortem`, `studyError`, `issue`, `issueError`, `fix`, `fixedBy` and `fixedAt` are null, `tries` is 0 and `acted` false. With nothing studied, nothing is acted on: no `What went wrong:` paragraph in the job's report, no `oby-failure` issue filed, updated or closed on GitHub, no `failure` inbox item, and `POST /v1/failures/{id}/fix` answers `409` `Oby hasn't studied this failure yet.` for every record it has (`404` for one it doesn't). When the post-mortem comes, filing its issue is an outgoing action: it goes through the publish gate (the secret scan on the title, the body and the arguments; nothing goes out if the scanner can't run) with the owner's `gh`, as publishing does. The post-mortem, acting on it and the fix job are #571.

### obid only: configuration

```sh
obid serve --config /path/to/config.json
```

```json
{"listen": "127.0.0.1:7811",
 "home": "/abs/path/obid-home",
 "owner": "wellington",
 "agents": {"claude": {"bin": "claude", "accounts": [{"id": "default", "name": "Default", "configDir": null}]}},
 "repos": [{"repo": "obi-e2e/fixture-app", "remote": "file:///abs/path/remote.git", "defaultBranch": "main", "check": "node --test"}],
 "allowFileRemotes": false,
 "bundledPlugins": "/abs/path/obi/plugins",
 "web": "/abs/path/obi/apps/web/dist",
 "telemetry": false}
```

- `listen`: an address on 127.0.0.1. obid binds only that, and refuses with `403` every request whose `Host` isn't `127.0.0.1`, `localhost` or `[::1]` (with or without a port), as a Mac runner does. Required, but on macOS, where it is `127.0.0.1:7777` when left out: where the Mac app looks for the runner on the same Mac.
- `home` (required, absolute): everything obid keeps, and the only place it writes besides its `HOME` and `TMPDIR`: its database, the API token (`api-token`, 0600), the accounts the API changed (`accounts.json`, 0600, and the folders it makes, `accounts/<id>`), the Obi key (`keys/`, 0600), clones (`clones/<owner>/<name>`), job worktrees (`worktrees/<job id>`), plug-ins (`plugins/`, `plugin-data/`), and the settings the API changes: `settings.json` (`maxRunning`, `PUT /v1/settings/jobs`) and `permissions.json` (the permission settings and the saved rules: `PUT /v1/settings/permissions`, `/v1/permissions/rules`), each kept across restarts.
- `owner` (required): the owner's id, as their Sphere knows them.
- `agents.claude.bin`: the Claude Code adapter's binary, a path or a command name found on the login shell's `PATH` (AGENTS.md, "PATH"). `accounts`: each `{id, name, configDir, payer}` (`payer` optional); they are the seed the accounts API changes ([obid only: accounts](#obid-only-accounts)); `configDir` is the account's folder, where its login is kept (and, on macOS, what names its Keychain item), or null for the tool's own default login; no box ever gets the folder itself: every run gets a folder of its own with a copy of the login ("The job sandbox", #655). `GET /v1/status` sends each one with `label: null` and `signedIn` true when its login file is there (#626): Claude Code's `.credentials.json` in its `configDir`, or `~/.claude/.credentials.json` when `configDir` is null; Codex's `auth.json` in its `configDir`, or `~/.codex/auth.json`. obid only looks whether the file exists (a regular file, not a link), never at what it holds. A Claude Code login only in the macOS Keychain counts when `claudeKeychain` is on, since obid then copies it where a run reads it (The job sandbox, under obid only: Obi-to-Obi review jobs); with it off it doesn't, since the job sandbox denies the Keychain. Cursor's accounts stay `signedIn: true`: a job whose tool says the login is missing fails saying so. Routing (a job with no `account`, the brain's dispatches and reviews) takes the first signed-in account of the tools in order (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor), so with `agent` left out a job goes to Codex when no Claude Code account is signed in; when none is, it takes the first account as before, and the tool's own words say why it can't run. A job naming its account runs there. Settings › Connectors in the web app shows such an account as not checked, never as connected. Codex and Cursor take the same shape when their adapters land (`CODEX_HOME`, `CURSOR_CONFIG_DIR`).
- `repos`: the repositories jobs may work in. `remote` is an `https://` or `ssh` URL, or a `file://` URL only when `allowFileRemotes` is true (the e2e test's fixture). `check` is the one-line command that decides `tests_pass` and serves as the job's check.
- `bundledPlugins` (absolute): the folder of the plug-ins obid ships, one `<id>/` folder each with its `obi-plugin.json` (this repository's `plugins/`). At every start, before the plug-in host starts, obid copies each of them into `home/plugins/<id>` when `home/plugins/<id>` is missing (regular files and folders only, through a temporary folder renamed into place), and installs it **disabled**: nothing is written to `home/plugins.json`. A folder already in `home/plugins/` is left as it is, and so is whether the owner enabled it. Left out, it is the `plugins/` folder of the repository obid was built from (`core/crates/obid/../../../plugins`, fixed when obid is compiled); when that folder isn't there (an obid copied elsewhere), obid installs nothing, logs why, and runs as usual.
- `web` (absolute): the built web app obid serves at `/app/` ([obid only: the web app](#obid-only-the-web-app)), `apps/web/dist` after `npm run build` in `apps/web`. Left out, it is that folder of the repository obid was built from (`core/crates/obid/../../../apps/web/dist`, fixed when obid is compiled); when it holds no `index.html`, `/app/` answers `404` saying the web app isn't built.
- `telemetry`: false unless the owner turns it on. obid sends nothing to anyone otherwise.
- `vault` (optional): where the owner's vault is ([obid only: the vault](#obid-only-the-vault)): `{"store": "file"}` for `home/vault/secrets.json`, or on macOS `{"store": "keychain"}` for the Swift runner's Keychain items, with an optional `"service"` in place of `com.deskree.oby.runner.vault` (tests use a throwaway one). Left out, it is the Keychain when `home` is the Mac app's runner home on macOS (compared with links resolved and letter case ignored), and the file otherwise. `"keychain"` anywhere but macOS is refused, and so is a blank `service` or one with spaces around it.
- `turnBudget`: how long one conversation turn may plan and act, in seconds, 1 to 110; 110 when left out (obid only: the brain).
- `orchestratorDelay`: how long the event orchestrator waits for nearby events to come together before its turn, in seconds, 1 to 600; 30 when left out (obid only: the brain, "The event orchestrator").
- `gateway`: where paired phones reach obid, an address on `127.0.0.1` like `127.0.0.1:8810`; no gateway when left out (obid only: the gateway).
- `claudeKeychain`: `true` lets obid copy Claude Code's login from the macOS Keychain where a boxed run reads it, only what calls the API, and have the Mac's own Claude Code refresh the default login in its last 5 minutes ("The job sandbox", below); `false` when left out. Only on macOS; elsewhere it does nothing. obid reads the Keychain with `/usr/bin/security`; its own tests set `OBID_SECURITY_BIN` to an absolute path of a stand-in, so a test's obid never reads the person's real Keychain.
- An account's `configDir` (any tool) is never one that holds obid's `home`, nor anywhere inside `home` but a folder of `home/accounts` (where the accounts API makes them): obid's home holds its database, clones and the jobs' worktrees.
- `conversation` (optional): how conversations run (obid only: the conversation harness). `agent`: `"claude"` (each conversation is a Claude Code session, the default), `"codex"` (each is a Codex thread) or `"plan"` (obid's plan loop answers, as before). `model`: the agent's model for conversations, `claude-sonnet-5-5` on Claude Code and Codex's own on Codex when left out. `budget`: how long one turn may take, in seconds, 10 to 3,600; 600 when left out. `turnBudget` stays the plan loop's.

**What Settings changes** is kept in `home` as files, not in the database: the permission rules and Settings › Permissions' default mode (`GET`/`PUT /v1/settings/permissions`, `/v1/permissions/rules`) in `home/permissions.json`, and `maxRunning` (`PUT /v1/settings/jobs`) in `home/settings.json`, both 0600 and kept across restarts.

The API token is the one in `home/api-token` (0600, at least 32 visible characters): obid makes it on first start, or takes the one put there before it starts. The shells on the same machine read it there. It is never in obid's environment or arguments, where any process of the same user can read it (on macOS through `KERN_PROCARGS2`, which no sandbox holds back; #439): obid refuses to start when `OBID_API_TOKEN` is set, saying to put the token in that file instead. The same holds for every secret obid holds: none is ever in its environment or arguments, nor in a child's. Every request sends `Authorization: Bearer <token>`; anything else is `401`. The one exception is reading the web app's own files under `/app/`, which hold no data ([obid only: the web app](#obid-only-the-web-app)); nothing under `/v1` takes anything but the token. Every `/v1` answer carries `Cache-Control: no-store` (unless it sets its own, like the event stream's `no-cache`) and `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`. `GET /v1/status` adds `"engine": "obid"` to `runner`. On SIGTERM or SIGINT obid stops taking requests, leaves its jobs as a restart would (After any restart, above) and exits 0 within 10 seconds.

**The Mac app on the same Mac (#440).** For Settings › Runner › This Mac, set obid's `home` to
`~/Library/Application Support/Oby/Runner` (expanded to an absolute path in the JSON) and leave
`listen` out, or use `127.0.0.1:7777`. This is `LocalRunner.home`, also where the app opens device
pairing. The app reads `api-token` there directly: no token in a process's environment or
arguments, no copy in UserDefaults, and no change to how the app starts. The file must be a
regular file owned by the current user with no group or other permissions (normally 0600),
at most 4096 bytes, and contain at least 32 visible ASCII characters with only optional trailing
CR/LF. Links, directories and malformed or exposed files are ignored. The existing Swift runner
continues using its keychain item (`com.deskree.oby.runner`, account `token`); that credential is
the fallback when no usable obid file exists or the server refuses its token with 401. A refused
credential is refreshed, so a stale file does not prevent connecting to a running Swift runner,
and rotating obid's token does not require restarting the app. Other obid homes and ports remain
valid for other clients; This Mac mode uses the fixed home and address above.

**Installing it (#585).** `install/install.sh` makes `home` (0700) and `home/api-token` (0600), writes this configuration (or keeps one already there, and then uses its `home` and `listen`), and runs obid as a launchd agent (macOS: label `com.deskree.obid`, home `~/Library/Application Support/Oby/Runner`, the This Mac home above) or a systemd user service (Linux: `obid.service`). With `--service`, `--prefix`, `--home`, `--config` and `--listen` it installs a second obid beside the first without touching it. The token is never in an argument, an environment variable, the service file or the configuration. docs/install.md also says how to update, uninstall and migrate from the Swift runner. **On a Mac, Oby.app runs obid itself** (2026-10-18): `Contents/Helpers/obid`, registered as the login item `com.deskree.obid` with "This Mac", started as `obid serve` with no `--config` (macOS only: this configuration's file, `~/Library/Application Support/Oby/obid/config.json`, written the first time with `listen` 127.0.0.1:7777, this `home`, the login's name as `owner`, Claude Code and Codex when found, `gateway` 127.0.0.1:8810 and `telemetry` false; its log `~/Library/Logs/obid.log`), with `bundledPlugins` and `web` defaulting to the app's `Contents/Resources/obid/plugins` and `web`. An install.sh launch agent of the same label keeps running until the app moves it (docs/install.md).

### obid only: accounts

The owner adds, renames and removes the agents' accounts, and signs them in, from the apps (Settings › Accounts). The configuration's `agents.<tool>.accounts` are the seed. What these endpoints change is kept in `home/accounts.json` (0600) and laid over the seed at every start; the configuration file is never rewritten.

- **When a change applies.** Every change reaches routing, the brain and the gateway's scrubbing at once, and every app following `GET /v1/events` gets `accounts` (the whole list, as `GET /v1/status` has it).
- **Checks at start.** Each entry of `home/accounts.json` is checked as the API checks it, and one that isn't right is left out and logged: an id obid didn't make (eight lowercase hex digits: macOS ignores letter case, so two ids differing only in case would share a folder), a folder other than `home/accounts/<id>`, a name or payer the API would refuse, a name (added or changed) another account of the tool has, a change for an account obid doesn't have, or past 32 accounts.
- **A file that doesn't read.** obid logs why, runs with the configuration's accounts, and answers `503` to every change until the owner fixes or removes the file.
- **No tool comes back on its own.** A tool whose accounts were all removed is gone, rather than back on its own default login (the person's), which an empty `accounts` otherwise means. Its default login is listed, as `default` (Claude Code) or `codex` (Codex), once anything about the tool's accounts changed, and can be removed like any account.
- **Not on the gateway.** None of these endpoints is on the gateway's allowlist: a paired phone's token must never add an account or sign obid into someone else's login, which would send the owner's code to that account. The Mac app calls them over loopback.

**`POST /v1/accounts`** adds one:

```json
{"agent": "codex", "name": "Personal", "payer": "Wellington"}
```

- `agent` is `claude`, `codex` or `cursor`. A tool the configuration doesn't name gets one while it has an account, run as `claude`, `codex` or `cursor-agent` found on the login shell's `PATH`. A tool whose configured `accounts` is empty keeps its default login beside what's added.
- `name` and `payer` are each one line of 1 to 60 characters, trimmed. `payer` is optional: null or empty means none.
- No two accounts of a tool have the same name, nor a name that is another one's id, letter case aside: jobs name an account by either.
- obid makes the folder, `home/accounts/<id>` (0700), and removes it with the account. The API names no folder: a body with `configDir`, or any other key, is `422`. obid makes the `id` (eight hex digits). At most 32 accounts, the configuration's among them.
- `201` with the Account; `422` with the reason for a wrong body.

**`PATCH /v1/accounts/{id}`** with `{"name": "…"}`, `{"payer": "…" | null}` or both answers the Account. Errors are `422` as above, and `404` for an unknown account. The configuration's accounts can be renamed and given a payer too.

**`DELETE /v1/accounts/{id}`** answers `204`.

- A sign-in under way ends first; `409` (`Personal's sign-in didn't end within 5 seconds: try again in a moment.`) when it doesn't within 5 seconds.
- `409` while a job of the account is queued or running: `Personal has a job queued or running: stop it, or move it to another account, first.`
- Its folder is removed only when obid made it (a real folder, never through a link). A configuration account that is removed stays removed across restarts. Its id is never made again, and its folder (one obid didn't make stays) is still hidden from every box, kept in `home/accounts.json` (`retired`).
- What the tool itself keeps stays: on macOS, Claude Code's Keychain item for the folder (Keychain Access removes it), and the login on the provider's side (signed in with Anthropic, OpenAI or Cursor until the person signs it out there).

**`GET /v1/accounts/{id}/sign-in`** runs the tool's own login for the account, never obid's (AGENTS.md: obid never reads, stores or forwards the login), and answers what the person needs to finish it:

```json
{"account": "a1b2c3d4", "url": "https://claude.com/cai/oauth/authorize?code=true&…", "userCode": null,
 "pasteCode": true, "expiresAt": "2026-10-18T10:15:00Z"}
```

- **What the app does.** It opens `url` in the browser. `userCode` (Codex) is the one-time code the person types on that page. `pasteCode` (Claude Code) means the page ends by showing a code, which the app sends back with `POST`.
- **One at a time.** Asked again while it's under way, the answer is the same sign-in; there is one per account. It ends after 15 minutes (Codex's code lasts that long), or once it has signed in.
- **Jobs wait.** While a sign-in is under way, and while an account is being removed, no job is dispatched to the account (a job naming it is refused: `Personal is being signed in or removed: try again in a moment.`; routing passes it by) and none starts on it: a job queued on it in the meantime stays queued and starts once the change ends. That holds from the moment no job of it was queued or running, and both are looked at under the same lock. An abandoned sign-in is ended with `DELETE` (below), rather than keeping the account's jobs waiting until it runs out.
- **Where it runs.** The login runs in the account's own folder: no box ever gets that folder (#655, "The job sandbox"), so nothing a job wrote can steer the login. The tool writes the login there itself, and obid never reads or copies what it writes there except for the boxed runs' copies.
  - **Claude Code:** `claude auth login` with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` set to the account's folder. On macOS the login goes to that folder's Keychain item, the one obid copies for a boxed run with `claudeKeychain`, so on macOS it's `409` unless `claudeKeychain` is on. Without a terminal, Claude Code prints a link whose page shows the code, and reads the code on its stdin.
  - **Codex:** `codex login --device-auth -c cli_auth_credentials_store="file"` with `CODEX_HOME` and `HOME` set to the account's folder. It prints a link and a one-time code, and listens on no port (its browser login needs `localhost:1455`). It ends by itself once the person has signed in.
  - **Cursor:** `cursor-agent login` with `NO_OPEN_BROWSER=1`, the account's own `HOME`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` and `CURSOR_CONFIG_DIR`, and `AGENT_CLI_CREDENTIAL_STORE=file`, so its login is a file under the folder rather than the macOS Keychain (jobs run with the same setting and get a copy of that file). Its caches (`NODE_COMPILE_CACHE`, `XDG_CACHE_HOME`) go in obid's folder for the sign-in, and its `cli-config.json` is rewritten first, as before a job. It ends by itself once the person has signed in.
- **How it runs.** Every login runs unboxed, since it must reach the Keychain and the network:
  - by its absolute path (`503` when obid can't find one);
  - with its environment made from scratch: the kept variables, `PATH=/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin` plus the folder of the tool's own binary (links followed: Cursor runs the `node` beside it) and, for a script that starts `#!/usr/bin/env <program>` (a CLI installed with npm: `#!/usr/bin/env node`), the folder of that program as obid finds it on the login shell's `PATH`, no token, and no Claude Code or Anthropic variable;
  - with `BROWSER=true`, so nothing opens on the runner;
  - with `TMPDIR` and its working folder set to a new folder of obid's (`home/tmp/sign-in-…`, removed when it ends, and swept at obid's next start);
  - in a process group of its own, which obid kills when the login ends, when the sign-in runs out, is ended or the account is removed, when obid stops, and when the request that started it ends before the login gave its link (its folder removed with it).
- **What obid takes from its output.** What a login prints is read, at most 4,096 bytes of each line, only for the link and the code:
  - the link is the first `https://` link on the tool's own sites (`claude.com`, `claude.ai`, `anthropic.com`; `openai.com`, `chatgpt.com`; `cursor.com`, `cursor.sh`, or below them), with no login, port or space in it;
  - the code is Codex's, a line like `3U0G-DCVSU`.
  Nothing else it prints is kept, answered or logged. obid's log says only that a sign-in started and whether it signed the account in.
- **Errors.** `404` for an unknown account. `409` in four cases:
  - the tool's own default login (an account without a folder: it is the person's, signed in with the tool itself), and a folder whose login file or its lock (Claude Code's `.credentials.json…`, Codex's `auth.json…`, Cursor's `.cursor/auth.json` or `.config/cursor/auth.json` and the folders on the way) is a link or not the person's, since the login would write the new sign-in wherever it leads;
  - a job of the account is queued or running;
  - the account is being removed;
  - Claude Code on macOS without `claudeKeychain`.
  `502` when the login gave no link within 30 seconds.

**`POST /v1/accounts/{id}/sign-in`** with `{"code": "…"}` sends the code the page showed to Claude Code's login, on its stdin, once. The code is never kept or logged.

- It answers the Account once it's signed in.
- `422` when the code isn't one line of at most 4,096 characters, or when Claude Code didn't sign in with it (`Claude Code didn't sign in with that code. Start again for a new link.`: the sign-in has ended).
- `409` when no sign-in is under way, a code went to it already, or the tool takes no code (Codex, Cursor).
- `504` when it didn't end within 60 seconds.

**`DELETE /v1/accounts/{id}/sign-in`** ends the account's sign-in under way, with everything its login started: `204`, also when none was under way. `404` for an unknown account; `409` when it didn't end within 5 seconds.

When a sign-in ends, every app gets `accounts`, with `signedIn` as obid finds it (the login file, or the Keychain item with `claudeKeychain`).

### obid only: the gateway

obid serves the LAN gateway's pairing and forwarding (LAN gateway (#284), above) as the Swift runner does, with these differences (`core/crates/obid/src/gateway.rs`, #446):

- **Where it listens.** Only where the configuration's `gateway` says, an address on `127.0.0.1` like `127.0.0.1:8810`; with no `gateway` there's none. Any other address is refused when obid starts (`gateway is "0.0.0.0:8810": it must be an address on 127.0.0.1 with a port…`), and a busy one stops obid before it serves anything. Once it listens, obid prints a second line on stdout after its first, `obid gateway listening on http://127.0.0.1:8810`. It isn't advertised over Bonjour: phones reach it through the owner's tailnet, with `tailscale serve --bg --https=8443 http://127.0.0.1:8810` (never `tailscale funnel`, which would put it on the internet), and pair at `https://<the Mac's tailnet name>:8443`.
- **Invitations** come from `POST /v1/pairing/invite` on obid's API with its API token, as above. `name` defaults to the machine's name and `hostname` to the `url`'s host and port. A Tailscale hostname is a link's `hostname` as it is, port included (`mac.tail0000.ts.net:8443`).
- **Paired devices** are kept, by their token's SHA-256 digest only, in `home/gateway-devices.json` (0600), not in `devices.json`: a phone paired with the Swift runner pairs again with obid.
- **Forwarding** happens inside obid: an allowed request with a paired device's token goes to obid's own API router with obid's API token and a loopback `Host`, carrying only its method, path, query, body (at most 4 MiB) and `Content-Type`. The answer has passed through the API's own layers, so the vault's secrets are already out; the gateway then nulls `session` and puts `[home]` for the accounts' `configDir` paths and for `home/logins` (where a job's copy of its login lives), as above, and sends only the status, `Content-Type`, `Cache-Control`, and an artifact's `Content-Security-Policy`, `X-Content-Type-Options` and `Referrer-Policy`. An answer that isn't text (not `text/…`, JSON or `…+json`: an artifact's image or PDF) goes byte for byte, as the API's own layer sends it. An answer of known length goes whole; any other (the event stream, `GET /v1/jobs/{id}/wait`) goes chunk by chunk as obid sends it, so a wait's first space reaches the phone at once. A stream ends at its first chunk after its device is revoked.
- **Redeeming** takes only what a phone sends: a request with an `Origin` (a browser) is `403` and one that isn't `application/json` is `415`, before it counts as a try, so a web page can't use an invitation up. A device's name keeps no control characters.
- **The allowlist** is Runner/Routes.swift's, plus `GET /v1/conversations`, `PATCH` and `DELETE /v1/conversations/{id}` (obid only: conversations), `GET /v1/artifacts`, `GET /v1/artifacts/{id}` and `GET /v1/artifacts/{id}/{version}/{path}` at any depth (obid only: artifacts), `GET /v1/jobs/{id}/report` and `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/proposals/{index}/file`. Routes obid doesn't serve yet answer `404` through the gateway as they do on loopback.

### obid only: the vault

A connection receives a credential from the same checked snapshot already learned by obid's
redactors. If Keychain Access changes an item between checks, the broker never gets a newer
credential from an unchecked second read. An unreadable store still refuses the request.


The owner's secrets for connections they make (#457), kept and used as the Swift runner keeps them (Engine/Vault.swift):

- **Where.** On a Mac whose `obid` runs in the Mac app's runner home (`~/Library/Application Support/Oby/Runner`, [The Mac app on the same Mac](#obid-only-configuration)), the Swift runner's own Keychain items: generic passwords in the owner's login Keychain under the service `com.deskree.oby.runner.vault`, the secret's name as the account, labelled `Oby vault: <name>`, so secrets the owner added to the Swift runner carry over. Anywhere else, and on Linux, `home/vault/secrets.json` (`{"secrets": {"<name>": "<value>"}}`) as the Swift runner's file vault keeps it: a folder only `obid`'s user may open (0700), the file readable only by that user (0600), each save a new file written 0600 and renamed into place under a lock (`home/vault/.lock`), so two `obid vault add` runs at once each keep their secret. The configuration's `vault` can say either ([obid only: configuration](#obid-only-configuration)). No items, or no file, is an empty vault, which everything reads.
- **What the Keychain is and isn't.** `obid` finds the login Keychain by its path from the user database, never through `$HOME`, and makes items with the Swift runner's service, account, label and accessibility (`WhenUnlockedThisDeviceOnly`). Measured on a Mac (2026-10-17): another program of the same user, even an unsigned one, reads and updates these items without being asked, and macOS adds it to the item's access list, so against such a program the Keychain holds them no better than the 0600 file does. That holds for the Swift runner's vault too, and is [#577](https://github.com/obi-ai-bot/obi/issues/577). Every new build of `obid` that reads an item is added to its access list again, so Keychain Access shows one entry per build: harmless, but expected. Only the program that made an item may delete it, so `obid vault remove` of a secret the Swift runner added fails, saying to remove it there or in Keychain Access.
- **Putting secrets in.** On the machine, as `obid`'s user: `obid vault --config <path> add NAME` takes the value on stdin (typed without echo at a terminal, with echo back if the command is stopped, or piped in), `obid vault --config <path> list` prints the names, `obid vault --config <path> remove NAME` removes one. A value is never an argument (`add NAME VALUE` is refused with the usage line) or an environment variable, and nothing the command prints holds one. A name is 1 to 64 letters, digits, `.`, `_` or `-`, starting with a letter or digit; a value is 8 bytes to 64 KB, with the whitespace around a paste dropped. A common word or a weak pattern (`postgres`, `changeme`, `00000000`: fewer than 6 different characters, a common word with or without digits after it, or under 50 bits by its length and kinds of characters) is refused, since every push holding it would be refused and every answer would have it taken out. No endpoint takes or returns a value.
- **While obid runs.** `obid` reads the vault at start and again whenever it changed (the file's inode, size or time; each Keychain item's name and modification date), so a secret `obid vault add` puts there is taken out from then on, without a restart. A Keychain item's date has whole seconds only, so the Keychain's values are also read again at least every 2 seconds: a second change in the same second is taken out within that. A removed secret stops being taken out. At start the log says which store the vault is and how many secrets it holds, never a name or a value.
- **Answers.** Every `/v1` answer has the vault's secrets taken out of every string, keys too, plain and in every encoded form `obid` knows (base64, standard or URL-safe, at any offset in a longer run, or wrapped over lines as GNU base64 writes it, also behind a diff's `+` or a quote's `> ` and with the line breaks written as `\n` inside one JSON string; hex in either case; URL-encoded, a form's `+` for spaces too), each as `[vault: <name>]`; a string that still holds one some other way becomes that marker whole. An answer with no secret in it goes as it was, byte for byte. The event stream and `/wait` are scrubbed a frame at a time. When the vault can't be read, a request isn't carried out: nothing reaches an agent, the brain, a plug-in or another Oby (no job is made, no words join the conversation). The exceptions only stop work and send nothing anywhere: `POST /v1/stop-all`, `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/stop`, `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/dismiss`, and `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/permission` denying without words (`message` absent, null or blank); those are carried out, so the owner can always stop work. Either way the answer is then `503` with `{"error": "The vault can't be read, so obid answers nothing until it's fixed: <why>"}`, `<why>` naming the file, folder or Keychain service, never a value, and what to do (`run chmod 600 on it`, `move it aside, then add the secrets again with obid vault …`, `unlock the login Keychain`), and an open event stream ends. A check's bundle (`application/octet-stream`) and the web app's files under `/app/` go as they are.
- **Agents.** Every prompt `obid` hands an agent's turn (a job's task, the owner's words for it, a publish-fix round, an Obi-to-Obi review) has the vault's secrets taken out the same way, and so do the files an Obi-to-Obi review hands its agent (the request and the diff in `.obi/inputs/`). When the vault can't be read, the agent gets nothing: the job fails with `The vault can't be read, so obid gave the agent nothing: <why> Once it's fixed, tell the job to go on.`
- **What goes out.** The publish gate and every scan (`crate::scan`: publishing, Obi-to-Obi replies and review results) look for the vault's secrets in those same forms, in every file a push sends, read whole (base64 wrapped over lines joined), and in each commit message; when the vault can't be read, the scan can't run and nothing goes out (`publishError` `Not published: the secret scan couldn't run (the vault can't be read: …).`).
- **The log.** The log layer takes the vault's secrets out of plug-ins' lines with the other secrets `obid` holds, as `[redacted: vault <name>]`; when the vault can't be read, the values it read last are still taken out.

### obid only: conversations

Many conversations with Oby instead of one thread (#493; decided 2026-10-16, D2): each thing asked on Home can start its own, and every device sees them all. The Swift runner keeps its one thread.

A conversation, as `GET /v1/conversations` lists it and the `conversation` event sends it:

```json
{"id": "5b0e2c4d-…", "title": "Review oby PR #33", "pinned": false,
 "created": "2026-10-16T09:12:00Z", "lastActivity": "2026-10-16T09:14:07Z", "cursor": "0-42"}
```

- `id`: a lower-case UUID. `title`: the first line the user said in it, as Sangam's design names conversations (`design/app/src/renderer/src/chats.ts`): at most 48 characters (the first 45 and `…` when longer), its first letter upper-case, any other control character a space. A rename replaces it. `created`: when it started. `lastActivity`: its latest turn's `at`, or `created`. Renaming or pinning isn't activity.
- `cursor`: its place in the list right now (pinned or not, and how recent its latest turn is), for paging (below). It changes with every turn and every pin. Treat it as opaque.
- Each keeps its last 500 turns, the oldest pruned, in a private file of its own (`home/conversations/<id>.json`, 0600).
- Every turn names its conversation: `GET /v1/conversation` and the `turn` event carry `"conversation": "<id>"`.
- **At most 200 are kept.** When a new one starts past 200 (and when obid starts with more), obid forgets the oldest that isn't pinned (the one whose latest turn is oldest), as `DELETE` would, with its `conversation` event. Pinned conversations and the latest one are never forgotten, so pinning more than 200 keeps them all. The jobs a forgotten conversation started stay.

**`GET /v1/conversations?after=&limit=`**: the conversations, the pinned ones first, then the others, each newest first (by their latest turn; obid orders turns exactly, not only to the second). `limit` is as for turns: 50 unless asked, 1 to 500. For the next page, send the `cursor` of the last conversation you show as `after`: the list goes on from that place. A cursor is a place, not a conversation: a conversation that stayed where it was is listed once, never skipped. One that moved since (a turn or a pin moves it up, an unpin down) can be missed or listed again; its `conversation` event already told you where it is, so keep one per `id`. `422` for an `after` that isn't a cursor. Fewer than `limit` means there are no more; `[]` before anything was said.

**`GET /v1/conversation?id=&before=&limit=`**: one page of the conversation `id`'s turns, as above; `404` for an `id` that isn't there. Without `id`, a `before` turn names the conversation (the one it's in); with neither, it's the latest conversation (the one with the latest turn), `[]` when there's none. So an app from before #493 shows the latest conversation and pages back through the one it shows.

**`GET /v1/conversations?q=watch target`** finds conversations by what was said in them (#593): a plain text match, no model. `q` is split on spaces, each word lower-cased with the punctuation around it taken off (`target?` is `target`, `off-limits` stays whole), and a conversation matches when its title, or one of its turns, holds every word, in any case. The list is in the same order as without `q`, with `after` and `limit` as above, and each conversation has one more key, `turn`: the latest turn that holds the words, `{"id", "who", "at", "text"}`, its `text` cut to 500 characters around the first word (`…` where it's cut), or `null` when only the title holds them. Only the conversations obid keeps are searched, so a deleted one never appears, and neither do turns pruned past the 500 kept. `422` for a `q` with no word in it or longer than 200 characters.

**`PATCH /v1/conversations/{id}`** with `{"title": "Vault questions"}`, `{"pinned": true}` or both renames or pins it, and answers the conversation. The title is trimmed: one line of 1 to 100 characters, with no line break (`\n`, `\r`, U+2028, U+2029) or other control character. `422` for neither key or a title that breaks those rules; `400` for a body that isn't that JSON (another key, a `title` that isn't a string, a `pinned` that isn't `true` or `false`); `404` for an id that isn't there.

**`DELETE /v1/conversations/{id}`** forgets it and its turns: `204`, `404` for an id that isn't there. The jobs it started stay, and keep its id in their `conversation`: show such a job's card on its own.

**`POST /v1/plans`** takes `conversation`, an `id` (the words join it; `404` if it isn't there), or `"new": true` (they start a new conversation, titled from them); both is `422`. With neither, the words join the latest conversation, or start the first one: the apps from before #493 (the Mac, the iPhone, the Watch) send neither and show one thread, and the Watch and the talk key carry on the latest conversation (#493). A screen that starts a conversation, like Home, sends `"new": true`. The brain reads only that conversation's turns (or the `turns` the app sends). Every answer, whatever its `kind`, carries `"conversation": "<id>"`, the one the words joined.

- A plan obid refuses is never a `422` (#546): obid tells the brain why and lets it try once more, then Codex plans, then keyword rules, which never act on the user's first words (they reply "Oby couldn't think that through: say it again", or ask which repository; to "continue", "go on" or "keep going" they reply "Oby couldn't think that through: say “continue” again in a moment.", never asking which repository, #632). The conversation keeps the words and that answer.
- New words in a conversation replace the turn under way in it, as `POST /v1/plans/cancel` would: its plan does nothing, and its request answers `none`. A turn in another conversation goes on.
- A conversation deleted while its turn runs stays deleted: the answer still comes, with its `conversation`, and Oby's words aren't kept.

**Jobs remember the conversation that started them.** A job a plan started has `conversation`, the id of the conversation whose words started it, kept when that conversation is deleted, and every `job` event of it carries it, up to the last one (how it ended): show its events in that conversation. A job from `POST /v1/jobs`, or another Obi's review, has `conversation` null. An inbox item names its `job`, so it belongs where its job does. Work that comes in from elsewhere (a routine, another Oby, GitHub) doesn't yet start a conversation of its own.

**A job tells its conversation how it ended (#351).** Each time a job a conversation started goes from `queued` or `running` to an end, obid adds one Oby turn to that conversation, with the job's card (`jobs` is `[<id>]`) and `"ended": true`, a key only these turns have. No model writes it: its first line is obid's own words, from the job's fields: `<title> is done: <pull request URL>` when it was published, `<title> is done, but it wasn't published: <why>` when its publish failed, `<title> is done.`, `<title> failed.`, `<title> was stopped.` or `<title> was interrupted.`, the title as `title` reads, cut to 80 characters. When the job's `summary` has a line, its first one (cut to 300 characters) follows on a second line: for a failed job, why it failed. Every secret obid holds is taken out of both, as `[redacted: <name>]`. The summary may be its agent's words, so the brain's briefing reads only the first line of such a turn; what the agent wrote stays in the quoted `work.md` (obid only: the brain). A job that ends again after Try again or one more round says so again. A job from `POST /v1/jobs` or another Obi's review (no `conversation`), a dismissed job, and what had ended when obid started add nothing; a conversation deleted meanwhile isn't told. This turn is apart from the event orchestrator's (obid only: the brain), which still reads a failed job and acts in its own conversation, `orchestrator`.

**Events.** A `conversation` event comes with the conversation as listed whenever that changes: when it starts, with each turn (before the `turn` event, so a new conversation is known before its first turn), and when it's renamed or pinned. Replace the conversation with that `id` and sort again. When one is deleted or forgotten, it's `{"id": "<id>", "deleted": true}`: take it out. An app from before #493 adds every `turn` to the one thread it shows, whichever conversation it's in, until it fetches again.

**From one thread to many.** When obid starts, the single thread it kept before #493 (`home/conversation.json`) becomes the first conversation, with all its turns. Its `id` is the thread's first turn's id when obid made that id (a lower-case UUID), and a new one otherwise; its `title` comes from the first thing the user said in it, and its `created` is the first turn's `at`. The conversation is written, the folder's entries are synced to disk, and only then is the old file moved aside to `home/conversation.json.pre-493`, unchanged, for a rollback to an obid from before #493 (rename it back). It isn't read again, and deleting the conversation doesn't delete it: delete it once a rollback is no longer wanted. A move cut short is finished at the next start, never made twice.

**Through the gateway.** On obid's gateway (obid only: the gateway, below), `GET /v1/conversations`, `PATCH` and `DELETE /v1/conversations/{id}` are on the allowlist, so a paired phone lists, renames and deletes conversations as the web app does. The Swift runner's gateway (Runner/Routes.swift) doesn't list them; `GET /v1/conversation` and `POST /v1/plans` are on both.

**On the Mac.** When the runner on this Mac is obid (`GET /v1/conversations` answers), the window's side panel lists the conversations, Pinned above Recent, and searches, renames (`PATCH`), pins (`PATCH`) and deletes (`DELETE`) them. A row opens that conversation (`GET /v1/conversation?id=`) and the box in it sends `conversation`. Home's box sends `"new": true`, and so do the first words of a New conversation, which opens empty. The talk key's words send neither, so they carry on in the latest conversation, unless the window is in front on the conversation page, where they join the one shown. Each conversation shows only its own turns. A job's page, and a task's menu, lead to the conversation named by the job's `conversation`, while that conversation is listed; deleted, the job has none to go to. The Mac follows the `conversation` event, and shows only the `turn` events of the conversation on screen. When the runner answers `404` to `GET /v1/conversations` (the Swift runner), or the runner isn't the one on this Mac (the Mac's own brain plans then), the Mac keeps today's single thread, hides the list and sends neither `conversation` nor `new`.

**Memory isn't shared between conversations yet.** What the user decides in one conversation ("never touch the Watch target") should hold in all of them (#493). obid's brain is briefed on the work (obid only: the brain, below), but the conversation it reads is only that conversation's own turns, and it has no memory that could carry a decision. Its remembered decisions (below) are the owner's answers to other Obis' requests, not what they say to Oby. Until Oby's memory (#378) comes to obid, a decision said in one conversation doesn't reach the others.

### obid only: the brain

obid's `POST /v1/plans` is the Swift runner's brain (Engine/Brain.swift, Engine/Conversation.swift's `Briefing`, `Desk.plan`), ported (#546), with what obid has:

- **The plan.** Every action of the schema, checked by obid with the Swift runner's refusal texts. A refused plan goes back to the brain with the reason and is tried once more; Claude Code retries a transient failure once (exit code 15, an empty answer, a timeout); quick questions think with Haiku (`claude-haiku-5-5`) at `--effort low`, requests longer than 600 characters and every later step with Sonnet (`claude-sonnet-5-5`) at `--effort medium` (#536); Codex plans when Claude can't, then rules. Those retries and that fallback are for the conversation's own turn, the one someone waits on: any other model call (a side call such as a title, a summary, a rewrite or a memory; obid makes none yet) is background, one attempt with Haiku within 15 seconds and never Codex, so during an outage it adds no load on the account the turn needs (#537). The brain thinks with each tool's first account. Unboxed (with nothing quoted to read), Claude Code never reads that account's `configDir`, which its jobs can write (#651): Claude Code applies a `.claude.json`'s `env` from its config folder whatever `--setting-sources` says, so a job could plant a `PATH` or plug-in folders that run outside the box. With a `configDir`, the brain runs by its absolute path with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` a new private folder in the call's own, holding only obid's copy of the login (the Keychain item's when `claudeKeychain` is on, else the folder's `.credentials.json` file; only what calls the API), and `PATH=/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin`; with the default login it uses `~/.claude` as before. A Claude account's `configDir` can't be `~/.claude` or inside it (configuration refused): that folder stays one no job can write. Each brain turn is logged and kept, the last 500, in `home/brain-turns.jsonl` (0600): the words, which adapter planned, the plan, and every failure and refusal before it, with the conversation, the user's turn and the step it belongs to (a turn of several steps has a record for each). It also keeps every model call the step took (`calls`, #533), a process retry, a retry after a refusal and a fallback to Codex each its own entry: `adapter`, `model`, `attempt` (from 1 within the adapter), `outcome` (`plan`, `refused` or `failed`), what Claude Code's result reported (`subtype`, `duration_ms`, `num_turns`, `total_cost_usd`, `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_read_input_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens`), and `wall_ms`, the time obid measured. A number the CLI didn't report is `null`, never 0; Codex's end of turn reports only its subtype. The log line of each turn ends with these numbers.
- **The briefing** is built from obid's own jobs, inbox and accounts. The conversation part is the last 50 turns, each cut to 500 characters. It carries obid's failure records of the last 7 days (obid only: failures, above; #591) under "## Failures this week": how many, counted by trigger (`failed`, `stopped at a limit`, `publish failed`), the rounds their jobs used in all, then one line each, newest first and at most 20 (`<id>: <trigger> · job <job> (<title>) · <repo> · ended <how long> ago · <n> rounds used`), with how many older ones it left out; with none it says `(None in the last 7 days.)`. What each job kept (obid's `what`, where it was, the end of its report and of its check) is quoted in `work.md` under "## Failure <id>", inside the turn's fence, with `Post-mortem: none yet. Oby hasn't studied this failure.` since obid studies none yet (#571). `read_job` on a job with failure records adds the same for its last 3 under "### Failure post-mortem" (`(no failure recorded)` without one). obid has no usage (each account says "no usage reported yet"), no model settings and no weekly reserves (#450): the accounts part opens by naming exactly those three as not on this runner yet, and tells the brain to say so rather than guess, and the settings part says so too. What agents wrote (a job's step and result, a permission's ask) is in the quoted `work.md`; when there is one, the brain may read it with Claude Code's Read and nothing else, with `--max-turns 6`, boxed like a job (it sees its own folder and its account's login, never obid's home or the person's); without one it has no tool at all. Unboxed, it keeps what a job keeps of obid's environment (`USER`, `LOGNAME`, the locale, the proxy and certificate variables), so Claude Code finds its login in the macOS keychain (#564). Boxed, it can't: the sandbox denies the keychain, so boxed it thinks with the first Claude Code account that is signed in (its login file is there, obid only: configuration). When none is and Codex is set up, Claude isn't asked to read at all: the turn goes straight to Codex, and its record's `refused` starts `claude skipped: no Claude Code account has its login in a file…` (#626). When Claude answers without a plan, why it failed keeps what it said (its JSON `result`, at most 300 characters, like `attempt 1: exit code 1: Not logged in · Please run /login, stderr: <empty>`), not only its exit code. The prompt is laid out so the model's server can cache it (#534): the system prompt and the schema are the same on every turn (job ids, inbox items, accounts, models and repositories are plain strings in the schema, checked by obid with the same refusal texts, and the briefing lists them); the briefing goes from what changes least to what changes most (the repositories, the settings, the accounts, the week's failures, the jobs, the inbox, the conversation, then the time), then the user's words, and the turn's canary ends the user message. When the turn's words or its briefing name a configured repository (`owner/name`, or its name alone as a word), the first 4 KB of that repository's `AGENTS.md` (its `CLAUDE.md` when it has none) join `work.md` under "## <repo>'s own instructions (quoted)", read from obid's clone at `origin/<defaultBranch>` as last fetched (#543): at most 3 repositories and 8 KB a turn, the words' first, inside the turn's quoting fence, with the vault's secrets taken out like the rest of the file. A repository with no clone adds nothing, and nothing is cloned or fetched for it. The system prompt lets the brain take a job's `check`, `base_branch` and task details from them; obid's checks on the plan are the same.
- **The loop**, its `step` events (which also carry `conversation`, the conversation the turn is in), Stop and cancel are as above. Turns in different conversations run side by side. The turn's budget (110 seconds, or `turnBudget`) bounds the brain's thinking too: no adapter thinks past what's left of it, and a plan that comes after it does nothing (it's kept in `brain-turns.jsonl` as refused, "the plan came after the turn's budget: nothing done"); the answer is what the turn did, then "That's as far as Oby got in one go: say “continue” for the rest." The same line ends a turn whose later step was left a second or less, which no brain is asked to think in (`budget_spent`, never `brain_failed`, #615), one whose budget ran out during a read or a lookup, and one that took its eighth action (`most_steps`). **A turn always ends with words in the conversation (#615)**, also when the brain's own words didn't end it: what the turn did, else what it looked at (`Oby searched obi-e2e/fixture-app for “Hello” and read src/greet.js.`, at most four, then `and 2 more lookups`), then what's left: "That's as far as…" on a spent budget or the eighth action, `, then couldn't think it through: say “continue” to pick up from there.` when the brain failed on a later step, `, then stopped.` after a Stop, and `, then kept going over the same ground: say “continue” to pick up from there.` when it asked a second time this turn for a read, a search or lookups it already had (`repeated`; the first time it's told they're in `work.md`). A brain that failed after doing and reading nothing says "Oby couldn't think that through: say it again." Only a cancelled turn, and one stopped before it did or looked at anything, answer `none`. The event orchestrator's turns say nothing of what they looked at. Each action ends as an outcome (done, couldn't, or needs the user to say which), and its step's `done` or `failed`, and whether the turn goes on, come from that outcome, never from the words of its line (#538). Each turn ends with a typed reason, which obid logs as `turn <id> ended: <reason>`: `answered`, `asked_user`, `started_jobs`, `could_not`, `needs_which`, `repeated`, `budget_spent`, `stopped`, `cancelled`, `brain_failed` (rules stood in), `most_steps` or `checked` (below). The answer itself is unchanged.
- **The completion check (#539).** Before a turn that did something answers (it started jobs or acted, and ended `answered`, `started_jobs` or `repeated`), obid lists what the user's words name: issue, pull request and epic numbers and repositories, read as the keyword rules read them, and the briefing's jobs by their titles (a repository named "oby" or "obi" isn't counted: it's how people talk to Oby). Anything the turn neither acted on nor mentioned in its answer goes back to the brain once, after what was done: "You haven't handled: #18. Plan it, or reply saying why not." That's one more step at most (it may come after the eighth); if it still isn't handled, the answer ends with "I didn't start #18." ("I didn't act on Vault secrets." for a job). The answer's `jobs` has the jobs of both steps. A turn whose brain failed, that was stopped or cancelled, whose budget ran out or whose action couldn't happen isn't checked, and neither is one that only answered a question. When the extra step goes on to another action or a read, the turn ends there (`checked`).
- **What waits for the owner's own Confirm** (the owner's decision of 2026-10-16). Nothing the brain reads may change a standing decision, so obid's own checks, not the prompt, hold back what persists or widens trust: `answer_permission` with `always` (it saves a rule) and `set_mode` to `bypass` (the job stops asking). The brain's plan becomes a `confirm` inbox item instead (Inbox item, above), and the turn says it waits in the inbox. Only the owner's own answer, `POST /v1/inbox/{id}/answer` with `Confirm` and the API token, carries it out; `Cancel`, `done` or `later` do nothing, and the brain is never offered a `confirm` item to answer. Allow once, no, and the other modes (`ask`, `plan`, and `acceptEdits`, which lets edits through but still asks before every command: the owner's choice) are done in the turn. So is `set_setting`'s `maxRunning` (#552): it's capacity, not trust. A job the brain plans in Bypass mode (a dispatch whose `mode` is `bypass`) doesn't start: it waits as a `confirm` item whose title obid makes from the job (what, the mode, the tool and account, the check), like `Start fix fixture-app (Greeting fix) in Bypass mode, on auto · auto, check node --test`. A `confirm` item doesn't expire; one whose target moved on (its permission answered, its job ended) is closed when confirmed, `409` with why, and its `text` says why.
- **Quoted data is fenced.** Each block of `work.md` sits between a line `<<<quoted-<nonce>` and a line `quoted-<nonce>>>`, the nonce random per turn and taken out of the data, so nothing quoted can close its block or fake another.
- **No vault secret reaches the brain** (obid only: the vault). Its prompt, system prompt, schema and `work.md` go through the vault first, every secret in it taken out in every form, as for an agent. When the vault can't be read, no brain runs (nor rules, in its place): the step's reply says `The vault can't be read, so Oby asked its brain nothing: <why> Once it's fixed, say it again.`, also between the steps of a turn already under way.
- **What a plan may do on obid.** Replies, questions and jobs (a job can't be an epic or read other repositories yet: obid refuses `epic` and `reads` rather than drop them, and a dispatch's `complexity` picks nothing on obid, which runs every job at its tool's own effort); `stop_agent`, `set_mode` and `answer_permission`; `retry_job` (obid has no failed-job inbox item: a job that failed, was stopped or was cut off is told "Try again, picking up where you left off", as `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/messages` would), `dismiss_job`, `answer_inbox` (any open item but a `confirm` one, with one of its options or the user's words; done in the turn only when it's one-off: `Allow`, `Deny` or the user's words on a permission, `Decline` on another Obi's request, `Cancel` on a `start` item, `Dismiss` on a plug-in event. `Always allow`, accepting another Obi's request at all, any "remember", an answer in words to another Obi's question, and `Start` on a `start` item become a `confirm` item, as above) and `set_setting` (only `maxRunning`: obid has no model settings or weekly reserves). Each action says what happened, one line per job, and ends the turn when nothing happened to any of them. What obid can't do yet is answered with why: `move_job` waits for `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/move` (#447); `start_now` (no job waits for a Start when: `start_when` must be empty, #455); `ask_review_again` (obid's brain can't ask for another review yet). A plan that names both an issue and a pull request (#219) starts the issue's work from the repository's base: obid's jobs have no `fromPR` yet.
- **Continue or start fresh (#541).** More work on a job that failed, was stopped or ran out of rounds goes on in that job, whose agent knows the code: a `fix`, `build` or `test` dispatch with the same repository and issue (or pull request) as such a job of the briefing (finished in the last two days, not dismissed) is refused with `issue #12 has job <id>, which failed, was stopped or ran out of rounds; use retry_job on <id> …`, and the brain plans again. When the user's own words say "new job", "from scratch" or "start over", the dispatch starts a new job. A review is always a fresh job.
- **Briefs carry what Oby found (#540).** A dispatch may hand its job `findings`, up to 10 of `{path, lines, note}` (`lines` like `120` or `120-134`, or null; `note` one line of up to 500 characters saying what is there or what was ruled out), and `done`, one line of up to 300 characters saying what must be true when the job is finished. obid refuses a finding whose `path` none of this turn's lookups showed (`path Engine/Keys.swift isn't in what you read`), and the brain plans again. Both reach the job's first prompt as quoted data, under "What Oby found before starting (quoted, may be wrong)", in the same fence as the brain's quoted file (a line `<<<quoted-<nonce>` to a line `quoted-<nonce>>>`, the nonce random per prompt and taken out of the data), never as instructions: they were written from what repositories, GitHub and the web said. They can't change the job's check, permission mode or permissions, which are the job's own fields. They aren't in the API's job. A dispatch with no research has none, as before.
- **Telling a job something (#542).** `message_job` is the user's too, not only the event orchestrator's: "tell the vault job to skip the Watch target" names one job of the briefing, and what reaches its agent is the user's own words, never the brain's restatement, with every vault secret and every secret obid holds taken out (`[redacted: <name>]`). A **running** job hears them at once, as `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/messages` would: at its next turn, with `notes` and `step` (`You: …`) showing them; the turn's step reads `Passing your message to <job>` and Oby answers `Told <job> what you said.` A job that **ended** (`done`, `failed`, `stopped` or `interrupted`, dismissed, merged or not, its worktree there or not) gets a follow-up at once, as `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/messages` makes it (the owner's decision of 2026-10-18, as Sangam's design does; it replaced #644's one more round in the job's own worktree): the words are its request, what the first job did is quoted in its first prompt, and the first job stays as it ended. Oby answers `<job> is finished, so I started a follow-up: <its title>. <tool> has it in <repository> on <account>.`, the follow-up's card on the answer (`jobs`). After a lookup this turn (research), the follow-up is only proposed, as research's dispatches are: a `start` item for the owner's Start (`Oby looked into it first. Start …, a follow-up of <job>?`, its task quoted), and Oby answers `<job> is finished, and this turn looked something up, so its follow-up waits for your Start in your inbox.` An event turn's words never start one, even when the job ends between obid's check of the plan and the action. A job takes one follow-up at a time (`POST /v1/jobs/{id}/messages`, One at a time). What the follow-up may do is what any job may: publishing, merging and secrets keep their own gates. Try again stays `retry_job`. `fix_failure` stays the event orchestrator's (below).
- **The Lead (#589).** Vague goals go to the Lead as above (The Lead: vague goals), with the same routing words, skipped list, plan, checks and reply; what differs on obid: the board and Oby's jobs aren't `.oby/context/` files but the Lead's `work.md`, each table in the turn's quoting fence and through the vault like the brain's file, which it reads with Claude Code's Read and nothing else (`--max-turns 6`, boxed like the brain's read), on Sonnet at `--effort medium`, with each tool's first Claude account and no Codex or rules standing in. It doesn't read the repositories' checkouts. Its call thinks for at most 120 seconds and never past the turn's budget; a plan that comes after it starts nothing. When the board has nothing workable, no model is asked: the reply names what was skipped and why (`Nothing to start on the roadmap. Skipped #1 (job 3f2a… is running on it), #4 (blocked).`). An issue in a repository obid doesn't work on is skipped (`obid doesn't work on <repo>`). Its jobs get no account by name (obid picks, as for any job) and no `lead` yet. Each issue is claimed as issue sync does it, with obid's own `gh`: `status:in-progress` and the hidden claim line at the end of its body, after the secret scan, never a comment; obid doesn't move the board's column yet. One turn at a time checks the live jobs and starts its jobs, so two turns at once start one job per issue. Every Lead turn is kept, the last 500, in `home/lead-turns.jsonl` (0600), apart from the brain's: `kind` `lead`, the conversation and turn, the goal, the words, the plan or why there was none, the skipped list, the jobs started, the issues claimed and the model calls.
- **The event orchestrator (#590).** What happens to a job goes to the brain first, as on the Swift runner (#351). obid builds the typed events itself from its own `job`, `check` and `inbox` events, each read as the job was when it was sent: a job that ends `failed` or `interrupted` (`failed`, `interrupted`), one whose account hit its limit (`limit`), a review error (`review_error`), a Mac or Apple check that ends `failed` or `error` (`mac_check_failed`), and an open inbox item that first shows (`inbox`; never a `confirm`, `start`, `permission` or `question` item, which are the owner's to decide already, or the orchestrator's own). What was there when obid started raises nothing, and neither does a dismissed job. It waits `orchestratorDelay` (30 seconds) for nearby events to come together, lets a user's turn go first, and takes at most 10 turns an hour. The turn runs in the conversation `orchestrator`, with no words of a person: the system prompt is the brain's with `fix_failure` added, and the user message carries a fixed policy and the events, inside the turn's quoting fence. No rules stand in for it. obid checks every plan of an event turn, not the prompt: it must read the event's job (`read_job`) before anything else; it may only take `retry_job`, `message_job` (its own words to the agent, about the error it read; to a job that ended it escalates, since that starts a follow-up and only the owner's own words start one), `answer_inbox` with `Try again`, `One more round`, `Rebase now` or `Wait for the reset` on an open item of the event's job that isn't a permission or a question, `reply_only` or `ignore`; every job it names must be one of the events'; and at most 3 automatic actions per job per UTC day, an action on a job in the same state as at the last one (the same failure again) escalating instead. `start_now`, `move_job`, `fix_failure` and `ask_review_again` are on the allow-list but obid can't do them yet, so they escalate with the action as Oby's recommendation, as does anything else (`ask_user` with Oby's diagnosis and recommended option, a dispatch, a dismiss, a setting). Escalating changes nothing and leaves one open `question` item, `orchestrator-<id>`, titled `Job <id> needs your decision`, whose text is why, with Oby's recommendation, and the events' summaries; no options, it's done when the owner settles it. A user's words in any conversation stop the event turn after the action under way (its turn ends `stopped`), and when it had done nothing its events are considered again on the next round. Its steps are `step` events with `conversation` `orchestrator`, and each step is kept in `brain-turns.jsonl` like the brain's.
- **Reading and looking up.** `read_job` and the research lookups are as above, with what obid keeps: a job's section has its status and step, its report, its pull request number, its last merge and Apple checks. It doesn't yet read what obid published or the pull request it follows (#530), and it doesn't read obid's failure records (obid only: failures, above) yet, so those parts say "(nothing went out)", "(no verdict yet)" and "(no failure recorded)". Code lookups use obid's own clone (`home/clones/<owner>/<name>`), fetched once per turn, at `origin/<defaultBranch>` unless the lookup names a ref. A `search_code` whose words no line has as one phrase is tried again with its words of 3 characters or more, the longest first, at most 3, until one is found (#632); its result then starts `No line has “gateway phone route allowlist”; these have “allowlist”:`. The same lookup twice in a turn runs once. A turn's lookups are kept for the conversation's next turn, the last 20 conversations' in memory (a restart forgets them): when that turn's words are "continue", "go on", "keep going" or "carry on", they're quoted again in its `work.md`, the brain is told what words they were for and to look up only what's still missing, the same lookups don't run again, and they don't count against the turn's 12 (#632). `view_issue` and `view_pr` use obid's own `gh` login; `fetch_url` resolves the host, refuses any private, loopback, link-local or Tailscale address, and connects only to the address it checked (`curl --resolve`), every redirect checked again; it reads only an address that's in the user's words or an earlier lookup's result this turn, and refuses any other whole (status `refused`, before it resolves or fetches anything), so a lookup can't carry out to the web, in a query, a path or a host name, what another one read. The address is never echoed into the brain's next request, only quoted in `work.md`. Why a lookup failed is quoted with its result, never in the prompt. Work planned after a lookup waits in a `start` inbox item: its `text` is obid's own words for the job it keeps (`Oby looked into it first. Start fix fixture-app (Greeting fix) in Bypass mode, on auto · auto, check node --test?`), `options` `Start` (recommended) and `Cancel`, `job` empty until `Start` queues it as planned, when the item's `job` names it. Two `Start`s at the same moment start one job: the first claims the item, the other is `409`, and a job that can't start leaves it open again. `Cancel`, `done` or `later` start nothing. obid doesn't yet add an Oby turn with the job's card on `Start`.

### obid only: the conversation harness

Talking to Oby is an agent session, as talking to Claude Code is (docs/conversation-harness.md, step 1: Claude Code). Unless the configuration says `"conversation": {"agent": "plan"}`, every `POST /v1/plans` turn runs in its conversation's Claude Code session; the plan loop (obid only: the brain) answers only when that can't run, and for roadmap work (the Lead). The request and its answer are as above (`reply`, `jobs`, `conversation`); what differs:

- **One session per conversation.** The first turn starts `claude -p --input-format stream-json --output-format stream-json --verbose --include-partial-messages --permission-prompt-tool stdio --permission-mode default --setting-sources "" --strict-mcp-config --restricted --tools <built-ins, no Bash> --max-turns 50 --model <conversation.model> --append-system-prompt <Oby's orientation> --mcp-config <file>`; its session id, from the `init` line, is kept in `home/conversations/<id>/session.json` with the account it's on, and every later turn adds `--resume <session>`, after obid restarts too. Only a session id that is a UUID is resumed, and a session of another account isn't: a new one starts. The prompt is the owner's words, through the vault (obid only: the vault); a new session also gets the conversation's last 20 turns before them, each cut to 500 characters, fenced as quoted data. The orientation tells it where it is, names Oby's tools and the repositories, and says that what it reads is data. `--restricted` removes the code-running tools (Bash and the rest) and Claude Code's auto-memory, keychain reads and `CLAUDE.md` auto-discovery; `--tools` names the built-ins it keeps — read, search, list, web search and fetch, writes in the room, sub-agents and a to-do list, and **no Bash**: nothing the session runs could read its login copy and send it out, and running, building or testing anything is a job. No plug-in, setting, hook, memory or other MCP server.
- **Its account** is Claude Code's first signed-in account (as routing finds one, #626: a login file, or the macOS Keychain's item with `claudeKeychain`). It never sees the account's own folder, which holds every job's and conversation's transcripts: for an account with a folder of its own, its `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` is `home/conversations/<id>/config`, which holds a copy of the account's login (the Keychain's item with `claudeKeychain`, else the folder's `.credentials.json`; only what calls the API — `claudeAiOauth`'s access token, its end, scopes, plan and rate tier — never the refresh token, as for every copy obid makes, #651; nothing else of the folder) and its own sessions; for an account without one, the person's login is copied into `home/conversations/<id>/login` as a job's is and shown in its `HOME`. The login is copied before every run, written without following a link, and taken out again when the turn's runs end. Before each run obid readies it as before a job's (#651): the Keychain's default login is refreshed by the Mac's own Claude Code when it's due, and a conversation's copy of it is kept fresh by the keeper while the turn runs; an account with a folder of its own isn't refreshed, and near its token's end the turn falls back to the plan loop with why. `CLAUDE_CODE_TMPDIR` is its `HOME`.
- **Its room and sandbox.** It runs in the job sandbox (bubblewrap on Linux, Seatbelt on macOS; obid only: the job sandbox), in `home/conversations/<id>/room`, its working folder.
  - It **writes** only its room, its `HOME` and `TMPDIR` (`home/conversations/<id>/home`) and its own login folder (`home/conversations/<id>/config` or `…/login`).
  - `room/repos/<owner>/<name>` holds a **read-only checkout** of each configured repository: the tree of `refs/remotes/origin/<defaultBranch>` in obid's clone (fetched once when there's no clone yet), extracted with `git archive` before the turn and again whenever that commit moved. `room/repos` stays read-only inside the room (Seatbelt denies writes under it after allowing the room; bubblewrap binds it read-only over the room, so it can't be renamed or removed either). A repository that can't be checked out is left out and logged.
  - It **reads** those, the tools on `PATH` (each tool's `bin`, `lib`, `libexec`, `share`, `etc`, `opt` and `Cellar`, never a whole install prefix that could hold the person's data), Claude Code's own install and the system's own folders, and nothing else of the machine's: it runs confined (`wrap_reading_only_its_own`, as an answer to another person's Obi does), so not obid's home (the database, `api-token`, the vault, the accounts' folders, other conversations, clones, job worktrees), the person's home, `/tmp` and `/private/tmp` (but the empty `/tmp/claude-<uid>` Claude Code opens), `/private/var/folders`, `/Users/Shared` or `/Volumes`. On macOS every path's metadata stays readable, never its contents.
  - It has the **network** (its model's API and the web). On macOS no Unix socket but the DNS resolver's and its own folders', no keychain, preferences, pasteboard, Apple events or LaunchServices, as for a job.
  - No code-host login (`GH_CONFIG_DIR` is empty, as a job's), no vault secret, no `GH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_*` or `CLAUDE_CODE_*` (but `CLAUDE_CODE_TMPDIR`) in its environment.
  - **Nothing planted carries over.** Before every run obid removes, without following a link, what the agent could have left for the next Claude Code to load: `CLAUDE.md`, `CLAUDE.local.md`, `.claude/` and `.mcp.json` in the room (and any nested `CLAUDE.md`/`CLAUDE.local.md` anywhere under the room but `repos/` and `attachments/`, whose files are the owner's, whatever their names), and `CLAUDE.md`, `CLAUDE.local.md`, `settings.json`, `settings.local.json`, `commands/`, `agents/`, `hooks/`, `skills/`, `output-styles/` and `plugins/` in its `HOME`'s `.claude/`, its `config/` and its login folders (`--restricted` and `--setting-sources ""` already keep settings and auto-discovery out; this is the backstop). The copied `~/.claude.json` keeps only the auth keys (`oauthAccount`, `userID`, the onboarding flags), never the person's projects, history or MCP state.
- **Its permission prompts** are obid's to answer, never the owner's: obid's fixed rules first (obid only: permissions answered without the owner: no push or post, nothing in the background, no file outside the room), then Sandbox mode's. Inside the room, yes; a write under `room/repos`, no (`repos/ holds read-only checkouts …`); web search and fetch, yes; Bash and every other code-running tool, no (they're not in `--tools` either); secrets, a login or the vault, anything that reaches outside the room, or a tool obid doesn't know stays inside, no, saying why and that a conversation never asks mid-turn. Oby's own tools (`mcp__oby__*`), yes; any other MCP tool, no. Nothing reaches the inbox.
- **Oby's tools**, the MCP server `oby` in its `--mcp-config` (a file in its `HOME`, written without following a link): `POST /v1/agent/mcp/conversation-<id>` with `Authorization: Bearer <token>`, a token made for the turn and forgotten when it ends, good only for that conversation's endpoint (`401` otherwise; on Linux it's reached at `10.0.2.2`, as the job broker is). It answers MCP's `initialize`, `ping`, `tools/list` and `tools/call` (JSON, not SSE). Each tool is one of the plan loop's actions, checked by `Plan.problems` against the briefing's ids as they are now (with the turn's own word as the canary no plan may repeat) and carried out by the same code, so its rules are the plan loop's: what persists, widens trust or starts in Bypass becomes a `confirm` inbox item only the owner's answer runs.
  - **A turn that read something untrusted** — a web search or fetch, a read of the repositories' checkouts or of the owner's attachments (from any turn), with Claude Code's own tools or Oby's `oby_read`, `oby_search` and `oby_list` (their code is data, not the owner's words; a search or listing with no path, a relative one, or one of the room itself counts, since it reads `repos/` too), a helper (`Task`, which reads as it likes), or `oby_job`, `oby_conversations` or `oby_inbox` (they return agent- and item-written text) — is treated as the plan loop treats research (#374): the jobs `oby_dispatch` plans wait in the inbox as `start` items for the owner's Start (the answer says `I looked into it first. A draft is waiting in your inbox; tap Start when you're ready.`), and the follow-up `oby_tell_job` starts for a job that ended waits the same way, a `start` item for the owner's Start. The mark is written to `session.json` as soon as it's set (and the session's id as soon as its `init` line names it), so a crash mid-turn doesn't lose it and a later resumed turn stays careful even if it reads nothing itself; a new session starts clean. A turn replaced while it waits for the one before it never starts. A turn that used only the owner's words and the room starts its jobs at once. A `start` item carries the planned task, quoted as data.
  - One turn calls `oby_dispatch` at most 10 times and `oby_tell_job` at most 10 times (the plan loop's most dispatches in a plan); past that the tool says `Oby starts jobs at most 10 times in one turn: …`.
  - `oby_jobs` `{status?, repo?, limit?}`: the jobs, newest first (20 unless asked, at most 100): `id`, `title`, `status`, `step`, `repo`, `agent`, `account`, `pr`, `published`.
