At most 2000 files and folders (hidden ones too), 32 folders deep and 50 MB per version, and 50 versions per artifact: past it, a new version is refused before anything is copied or scanned (`… has 50 versions, the most obid keeps; publish it as a new one.`). A job's artifacts hold at most 500 MB, every version of each counted: an entry that would take it past is refused, with the entries after it, in one line of `artifactProblems` (`3 entries: Not published: this job's artifacts hold 498 MB, and 500 MB is the most one job's may.`). A conversation's own artifacts (those with no `job`, from `oby_artifact`) have the same 500 MB between them: `Not published: this conversation's artifacts hold … MB, and 500 MB is the most one conversation's may.` Each file is checked again by its name after it's opened (`fstatat`, not following a link): another file in its place since (`… changed while obid copied it`) or a second link to it is refused. There is no cap on the store as a whole yet.
- **The secret scan.** Before anything is kept, every file goes through the publish gate's scan (`crate::scan`, obid only: publishing the owner's work): the built-in rules, the secrets obid holds and the vault's in every form it knows, and gitleaks when it's installed, on every file read as text whatever its bytes (bytes that aren't UTF-8 read as U+FFFD, so the ASCII a key is written in stays whole), and once more with its NUL bytes taken out when it has some, so UTF-16 text is read too. What a compressed stream holds isn't looked inside: a PDF's Flate streams, a PNG's `zTXt` or `iTXt` chunks, gzip or zip content. A finding, or a scan that can't run (the vault can't be read), and nothing is kept: `Not published: the secret scan found what looks like a secret in config.js line 1 (AWS access key).` Never the value.
- **Names are scanned too.** The path asked for, the title and every file's path inside the artifact go through the built-in rules, the secrets obid holds and gitleaks, each as a line of its own, and so does every part of each between `/`s and every piece of a part between `.`s (gitleaks finds a key only where a word ends, so `<key>.md` and `<key>/index.html` would hide it); one that looks like a secret, and nothing is kept. A name, a title or a refusal that holds a run of 32 or more letters, digits, `_`, `+` or `-` with both letters and digits in it is refused the same way, as a token joined to a name (`notes-<key>.md`, `notes~<key>.md`, `<key>@x.md`) gets past gitleaks; one of obid's own ids (a UUID, `8-4-4-4-12` hex) isn't, nor is a run of lower-case hex alone (a bundler's hashed name, `main.<32 hex>.js`). A refusal never repeats such a name: any refusal (`Err`, and each line of a job's `artifactProblems`) that holds what looks like a secret, read part by part the same way, is said as `Not published: a name in it looks like a secret, so obid doesn't say it.` instead, and a job's line for such an entry is that sentence alone, never with the entry's name before it. A file that starts like a PDF, a zip or a gzip is still read by gitleaks; one it sniffs as a tar or an ISO image further in makes the scan one that couldn't run, so nothing is kept (publishing the owner's work, above, says how).
- **For whoever writes the publishing call** (the conversation's `oby_artifact`): `Artifacts::publish(Publish {root, path, title, kind, conversation, job, artifact})` in `core/crates/obid/src/artifacts.rs` answers `Ok((id, version))` once the `artifact` event went out, or `Err` with one line starting `Not published: `. `root` is obid's own folder (the room), `path` the agent's, relative to it; `kind` `None` takes it from the shape, and one given must be it; `artifact` asks for a new version of that one, which must have the same `conversation` and `job` and the same kind, and keeps its title (the owner may have renamed it). `shared.artifacts` holds it.

**`GET /v1/artifacts?conversation=&job=`**: every artifact, newest first, or only a conversation's or a job's; `[]` when none.

**`GET /v1/artifacts/{id}`**: the artifact; `404` when there's no such one.

**`GET /v1/artifacts/{id}/{version}/{path}`**: one of the version's files, `path` as `files` lists it (`assets/site.css`); anything else (another name, `..`, a hidden name, a version that isn't there) is `404`. `Content-Type` by its extension (`text/html; charset=utf-8`, `text/css; charset=utf-8`, `text/javascript; charset=utf-8`, `text/markdown; charset=utf-8`, `image/png`, `application/pdf`…; `application/octet-stream` when unknown), and on every file:

```
Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'self' data: blob:; script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline' data: blob:; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline' data:; img-src 'self' data: blob:; connect-src 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'; sandbox allow-scripts
X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff
Referrer-Policy: no-referrer
```

A page's scripts run, but:

- it loads only its own files (any file of obid's served on the same address, but every one needs the token) and `data:`/`blob:` URLs: no other server, no font or script from the web;
- `connect-src 'none'`: no `fetch`, XHR, WebSocket or EventSource, not even to obid's own API;
- `sandbox allow-scripts`: the page has an origin of its own, not obid's, so it can't read what obid's other pages keep (the web app's token in session storage), its cookies, or a frame of the web app; it opens no window or popup;
- `object-src 'none'`, `base-uri 'none'`, `form-action 'none'`: no plug-in, no `<base>` sending its links elsewhere, no form sent anywhere;
- `frame-ancestors 'none'`: no other page can frame it.

**What the policy doesn't stop**, so this isn't "no network" by itself: the page navigating itself to another address (a link, `location = …`, `<meta http-equiv="refresh">`), WebRTC, and DNS prefetch. A CSP has no rule for those. What such a request can carry is only what the page holds, since it reads nothing else; the apps must deny every navigation and load it with network access denied (the client contract in docs/conversation-harness.md, section 4). Text files are also scrubbed of the vault's secrets as every `/v1` answer is (obid only: the vault).

**`PATCH /v1/artifacts/{id}`** with `{"title": "…"}` renames it and answers the artifact: `422` for a title that isn't one line of 1 to 100 characters, `400` for another body, `404` for no such artifact.

**`DELETE /v1/artifacts/{id}`** deletes it with every version: `204`, `404` for no such artifact. Jobs keep its id in their `artifacts`.

**Through the gateway** a paired phone lists and reads them (the three `GET`s), files byte for byte with their headers; renaming and deleting stay on the loopback API. A file that isn't text (an image, a PDF) passes the gateway unscrubbed, as the API's own layer passes it: nothing takes the vault's secrets or the accounts' homes out of it there. That's accepted: it was scanned before it was kept (above).

### obid only: the web app

`apps/web` (Sangam's screens, #492) is a client of this API like the Mac and iPhone apps, for a browser on the same machine: Home, task pages, the conversation and Settings, showing obid's jobs, conversation and inbox. obid serves its built files on its own loopback address, so nothing else listens and nothing new is reachable from the network.

- **The page uses the API as every client does: with the token.** Every call it makes, the event stream included, sends `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (the stream is read with `fetch`, since `EventSource` can't send a header). The page keeps the token in the browser's session storage for its own origin, which another port on 127.0.0.1 is not, so a local server the person opens can't read it; it's forgotten when the tab closes, on sign-out, and when obid refuses it (`401`), after which the page asks for it again. **No cookie reaches `/v1`**: a request there without the token is `401`, whatever cookie or `Sec-Fetch-Site`, `Origin` or other header comes with it, since anything on the machine can send those.
- **`GET /app/`** is the page; **`GET /app/{path}`** its files (scripts, styles, the mascot from `character/` as `oby/oby.html`), read from `web` (configuration, above). `GET /app` is `308` to `/app/`. Only plain names inside that folder are served: no `..`, no hidden files, nothing a link leads out of it to (`404`). Pages carry a `Content-Security-Policy` (the app's own scripts only; the mascot's page may run its inline ones), and every file `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` and `Cache-Control: no-cache`.
- **The page's files are behind the token too.** They hold no data, but #492 keeps the page itself closed without the token, and a browser can't send a header when it opens a page. So with neither the token nor a page session, `GET` of any `/app` path is `401` with a small sign-in form (nothing of obid's, one script), and `/v1/…` is `401` as always. A `Host` that isn't a loopback name is `403` first, as for every request.
- **Signing in.** The form posts the token once: **`POST /app/sign-in`** with `token=<the token>` (`application/x-www-form-urlencoded`; spaces and line ends around it are ignored). Its script keeps the token in the page's session storage on the way, so the page has it for the API, and sends it again by itself (once in 10 seconds at most) when the form comes back while the tab still holds it, as after obid restarted. The right token answers `303` to `/app/` and opens a **page session**: 32 random bytes in hex, in `Set-Cookie: obid-page=<value>; Path=/app; HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict` (no `Max-Age`: it ends with the browser's session). obid keeps only its SHA-256, in memory, at most 64 at once (another sign-in ends the oldest); it's nothing derived from the token. Anything else is the form again, `401`, with no cookie; so is a form sent from another site's page (`Sec-Fetch-Site` other than `same-origin`).
- **A page session opens only `GET` and `HEAD` of `/app` paths**, the page's own files, and nothing else. It ends when the browser signs out, when obid stops, or once 64 newer ones were opened. Browsers send the cookie to every port on 127.0.0.1 under `/app`, so a local server may receive it; all it opens there is files anyone can build from this repository.
- **`POST /app/sign-out`** ends the session the request's cookie names (none: nothing ends), clears the cookie (`Max-Age=0`) and answers `303` to `/app/`, the sign-in form again. Like signing in, it passes the gate on a loopback `Host` alone: it can only end a session. The page forgets the token first.
- What the page shows comes only from this API: `GET /v1/status`, `GET /v1/jobs`, `GET /v1/jobs/{id}`, `GET /v1/inbox`, `GET /v1/conversations`, `GET /v1/conversation`, `GET /v1/events`, `GET /v1/obid/plugins` and `GET /v1/connections`, and it acts through `POST /v1/plans`, `PATCH` and `DELETE /v1/conversations/{id}`, `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/messages`, `/stop`, `/dismiss` and `POST /v1/inbox/{id}/answer`. Screens with no endpoint behind them yet (the Obysphere, its knowledge) stay hidden behind a setting in the page and show nothing made up.
- **Settings › Connectors is read-only** (#518). It lists obid's accounts and its `github` login from `GET /v1/status`, its plug-ins from `GET /v1/obid/plugins` and its connections from `GET /v1/connections`, reading the two lists when the page opens and every 15 seconds while it's open. A `404` for a list means there is none (obid serves no connections before #444); any other answer is said under the page, never shown as an empty list. It turns nothing on or off and disconnects nothing: a connection is changed with a device's signature ([Connection](#connection)), which a browser has no key for, so each row says where it changes (a connection or a plug-in in Oby on the Mac, an account or the GitHub login on the runner's machine).
- **Many conversations, one on screen.** obid keeps many conversations ([obid only: conversations](#obid-only-conversations), #493). The side panel lists them all (`GET /v1/conversations`, paged by `cursor`, kept current by `conversation` events), pinned first, then newest first, and renames, pins and deletes them (`PATCH`, `DELETE`). The page shows one: the latest when it opens (`GET /v1/conversation` with no `id`), or the one picked in the list. Home's message box and New start one (`POST /v1/plans` with `"new": true`), which the page then shows; a reply sends the shown conversation's `id` (`conversation`), so the words never join another one, and the page keeps out every `turn` event whose `conversation` is another's. When the one it shows is deleted, it shows none, and its next words start a new one (`"new": true`). Words sent to a conversation deleted before the page heard of it are `404` and obid keeps none of them: the page takes it off the list, says so, and the next words start a new one.
- None of this is on the gateway's allowlist (Runner/Routes.swift): the web app is for a browser on the machine obid runs on. Reaching it from another device is the gateway's or a tunnel's, as for the API.
